<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> At every fixed inverse temperature <i>β</i> &gt; 1, we prove a stretched-exponential obstruction to mixing for the zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model from typical equilibrium configurations held fixed as initial states. The Gibbs mass of states whose total-variation distance from equilibrium at time $`\exp(n^{1/10000})`$ exceeds 1/4 tends to one in probability over the disorder. This holds for rate-one-per-site heat-bath dynamics and after the same stated number of discrete update attempts.

- **claim-main-stretched-exponential** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Set $\kappa=1/10000$. For every fixed $\beta>1$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb P_{J,\,x_0\sim\pi^J} \left\{d^J\bigl(x_0,\exp(n^\kappa)\bigr)>\frac14\right\} &\longrightarrow1,\\ \mathbb P_{J,\,x_0\sim\pi^J} \left\{d_{\mathrm{disc}}^J \bigl(x_0,\lfloor\exp(n^\kappa)\rfloor\bigr)>\frac14\right\} &\longrightarrow1. \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: From a typical equilibrium start held fixed, both the continuous-time and the discrete update dynamics remain far from equilibrium in total variation at the stretched-exponential time scale, with probability tending to one.
- **claim-superpolynomial-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-superpolynomial)): For every fixed $\beta>1$ and every fixed $a>0$, $$\mathbb P_{J,\,x_0\sim\pi^J}\{T^J(x_0)>n^a\}\longrightarrow1, \qquad \mathbb P_{J,\,x_0\sim\pi^J} \{T_{\mathrm{disc}}^J(x_0)>n^a\}\longrightarrow1.$$ Equivalently, each corresponding Gibbs mass tends to one in probability over the disorder.
  - Explanation: Every fixed polynomial time scale is eventually below the stretched-exponential one, so contraction of total variation gives a superpolynomial point mixing time with probability tending to one.
- **claim-gaussian-tools** (theorem, [source](#stmt-static-lem-gaussian-tools)): Let $G$ be a standard Gaussian vector in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space. If $f$ is $L$-Lipschitz, then for $a>0$ and $\theta\in\mathbb R$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb P\bigl[\|f(G)-\mathbb Ef(G)\|\ge a\bigr] &\le 2\exp\left(-\frac{a^2}{2L^2}\right), \label{static-eq:gaussian-concentration}\\ \log\mathbb E\exp\{\theta(f(G)-\mathbb Ef(G))\} &\le\frac{\theta^2L^2}{2}. \label{static-eq:gaussian-mgf} \end{aligned}$$ If a probability density on $\mathbb R^m$ is proportional to $e^{-V}$, where $V$ is twice differentiable and $\nabla^2V\succeq I$, then $$\label{static-eq:brascamp-lieb} \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(f)\le\mathbb E\\|\nabla f\\|^2.$$ In particular, the variance of a linear functional $a\cdot G$ under this density is at most $\\|a\\|^2$.
  - Explanation: Gaussian concentration and moment-generating bounds for Lipschitz functions, together with the Brascamp–Lieb variance bound for strongly log-concave densities.
- **claim-hierarchy-derivative** (theorem, [source](#stmt-static-lem-hierarchy-derivative)): Suppose an independent standard Gaussian vector $g$ at level $j$ enters the terminal exponent as $\sqrt v\,g\cdot e(S)$, where $e$ is a fixed vector-valued function of the terminal configuration. With all other parameters fixed, $$\label{static-eq:hierarchy-derivative} \frac{\partial}{\partial v}\mathbb EF_0 =\frac12\left( \mathbb E\\|e(S)\\|^2- \sum_{l=j}^k w_l\mathbb E_l^{(2)}e(S^1)\cdot e(S^2) \right).$$ A quenched Gaussian is treated as level $0$. The identity holds for $v>0$ and extends to interpolation endpoints by integration and continuity.
  - Explanation: Derivative of the hierarchy pressure in a Gaussian variance parameter, written through the self-energy minus weighted two-replica cross terms.
- **claim-matrix-interpolation** (bound, [source](#stmt-static-prop-matrix-bound)): For every feasible $D$ and every path and multiplier as above, $$\label{static-eq:matrix-bound} F_{d,n}(D)\le \Psi(Q,x,\lambda)-\lambda:D +\frac{\beta^2}{4} \left(\|D-Q_{\rm end}\|^2-\int x\,\,\mathrm d\|Q\|^2\right).$$ For fixed $D$ and $\lambda$, the bound passes to any sequence of finite paths for which $Q_{\rm end}$, $\Psi(Q,x,\lambda)$, and the displayed path integral converge.
  - Explanation: Upper bound on the constrained multi-replica pressure by a single-site hierarchy value, with an explicit quadratic cost for a free covariance endpoint.
- **claim-finite-size-pressure** (bound, [source](#stmt-static-thm-finite-size)): For every fixed $\beta>0$, there is $C_\beta<\infty$ such that for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\label{static-eq:finite-size} P_\beta-C_\beta n^{-1/24}\le p_n(\beta)\le P_\beta.$$
  - Explanation: The expected normalized log partition function lies below the scalar Parisi minimum and above it up to an explicit polynomially small error.
- **claim-field-energy-variance** (bound, [source](#stmt-static-lem-field-energy)): For every $0\le j\le k$, under the single-path law, $$\label{static-eq:field-energy-concentration} \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\left(\frac{z_j\cdot\sigma}{n}\right)\le\frac1n.$$
  - Explanation: The normalized Gaussian field energy along the hierarchy has small variance under the single-path law.
- **claim-average-overlap-concentration** (bound, [source](#stmt-static-lem-average-overlap)): Let $\Delta_{\min}=\min_j\Delta_j$. If $k\ge1$ and $k/\sqrt{n\Delta_{\min}}\le1$, then $$\label{static-eq:average-overlap-concentration} \sum_{j=0}^kw_j\mathbb E_j^{(2)}(R-r_j)^2 \le C\left(\frac{k}{\sqrt{n\Delta_{\min}}}+k^{-1/2}\right),$$ where $C$ is an absolute constant.
  - Explanation: The level-weighted overlap fluctuation around the hierarchy means is small when the field gaps are large enough.
- **claim-scalar-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-static-lem-scalar-regularity)): The step approximation defining $\Phi_\alpha$ is independent of the approximating sequence. The functions $\Phi_\alpha,u,\chi$ are continuous on $[0,1]\times\mathbb R$, with $$\label{static-eq:scalar-derivative-bounds} \|u\|\le1,\qquad 0<\chi\le1,\qquad \|\chi(s,z)-\chi(s,z')\|\le C_\beta\|z-z'\|.$$ The function $\Phi_\alpha(s,\cdot)$ is even, so $u(s,0)=0$. For two distribution functions $\alpha,\widetilde\alpha$, $$\label{static-eq:scalar-stability} \sup_{s,z}\bigl( \|\Phi_\alpha-\Phi_{\widetilde\alpha}\| +\|u_\alpha-u_{\widetilde\alpha}\| +\|\chi_\alpha-\chi_{\widetilde\alpha}\| \bigr) \le C_\beta\int_0^1\|\alpha(s)-\widetilde\alpha(s)\|\,\mathrm ds.$$ These functions are also continuous in $\beta>0$, uniformly in $(s,z)$ for their differences, locally in $\beta$.* *The stochastic differential equation $$\label{static-eq:scalar-diffusion} \,\mathrm dX_s=\beta\,\mathrm dB_s+\beta^2\alpha(s)u(s,X_s)\,\mathrm ds, \qquad X_0=0,$$ has a unique strong solution. For a step distribution function, its transitions across the constant-mass intervals are the normalized tilted Gaussian kernels of the scalar recursion. Its bounded martingale $U_s=u(s,X_s)$ satisfies $$\label{static-eq:scalar-martingale} U_t-U_s=\beta\int_s^t\chi(v,X_v)\,\mathrm dB_v.$$ Moreover, $$\label{static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation} \mathbb E\chi(t,X_t)-\mathbb E\chi(s,X_s) =-\beta^2\int_s^t\alpha(v)\mathbb E\chi(v,X_v)^2\,\mathrm dv.$$ For $0\le s<t\le1$, the conditional form is $$\label{eq:scalar-evolution} \mathbb E[\chi(t,X_t)\mid X_s] =\chi(s,X_s)-\beta^2\int_s^t\alpha(v) \mathbb E[\chi(v,X_v)^2\mid X_s]\,\mathrm dv.$$ Consequently $\Gamma(s):=\mathbb Eu(s,X_s)^2$ is continuously differentiable, with one-sided derivatives at the endpoints, and $$\label{static-eq:gamma-derivative} \Gamma(0)=0,\qquad \Gamma'(s)=\beta^2\mathbb E\chi(s,X_s)^2.$$ The well-posedness statement and the evolution identities <a href="#static-eq:scalar-martingale" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="static-eq:scalar-martingale">[static-eq:scalar-martingale]</a>–<a href="#static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation">[static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation]</a> also hold for a diffusion started at any deterministic time and spatial value.
  - Explanation: Well-posedness, derivative bounds, stability and the scalar diffusion evolution identities for the Parisi equation with a general distribution function.
- **claim-scalar-minimizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-static-prop-scalar-minimizer)): The scalar functional has a minimizer $\mu$. For every such minimizer, with its associated scalar process, the following assertions hold:* 1. *$0\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu$, and $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu\subset[0,1-\varepsilon_0]$ for some $\varepsilon_0>0$.* 2. *At every $q\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu\cap(0,1)$, $\Gamma(q)=q$ and $\Gamma'(q)\le1$. At $q=0$, $\Gamma(0)=0$ and $\Gamma'(0)\le1$.* 3. *Define $$\label{static-eq:scalar-A-d-definitions} d_*(s)=1-\int_{(s,1]}t\,\mu(\,\mathrm dt),\qquad A(s)=\int_s^1\alpha(t)\,\mathrm dt=d_*(s)-s\alpha(s).$$ Then, at every $s\in[0,1]$, $$\label{static-eq:scalar-stationarity-identity} \mathbb E\bigl[\chi(s,X_s)+\alpha(s)u(s,X_s)^2\bigr]=d_*(s).$$ Equivalently, since $d_*(s)=A(s)+s\alpha(s)$, $$\label{eq:scalar-stationarity} \mathbb E\chi(s,X_s)=A(s)+\alpha(s)(s-\Gamma(s)) \qquad(0\le s\le1).$$ In particular $$\label{static-eq:scalar-susceptibility} \chi(0,0)=A(0),\qquad \beta A(0)\le1.$$ Furthermore $\alpha(s)>0$ for every $s\in(0,1]$, $A(h)<A(0)$ for every $h\in(0,1]$, and $d_*(0+)=A(0)$.
  - Explanation: A minimizer of the scalar functional exists; its support contains zero and stays below one, and it satisfies stationarity and susceptibility identities.
- **claim-energy-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-static-lem-energy-gap)): For each fixed $\beta>1$, there are $e_0=e_0(\beta)<\beta/2$ and $N=N(\beta)$ such that $$\label{static-eq:energy-gap} p_n'(\beta)=\frac1n\mathbb E\pi_{n,\beta}^J(H_n^J)\le e_0 \qquad(n\ge N).$$
  - Explanation: Above the critical inverse temperature the equilibrium energy density is strictly below its trivial bound for large system size.
- **claim-signed-fixed-locking** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fixed-locking)): Let $e_*=1/100$. For every fixed $h\in(0,1)$, there is $b>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\label{eq:fixed-locking} \mathbb E\pi^{\otimes3}\!\left\{ \begin{gathered} R(x,y)\ge h,\qquad \|R(v,x)\|,\|R(v,y)\|\le b,\\ \|R(v,x)-R(v,y)\|>2n^{-e_*} \end{gathered}\right\}\le e^{-n^{9/10}}.$$ The same conclusion holds after decreasing $b$.
  - Explanation: If two replicas have a substantial overlap, their small signed overlaps with a third replica are locked together except on an exponentially small event.
- **claim-pressure-gap-probability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pressure-prob)): Fix a replica number $d$. If a feasible overlap matrix $D$ satisfies $$F_{d,n}(D)\le dP_\beta-\eta, \qquad \eta\ge2dC_\beta n^{-1/24},$$ where $C_\beta$ is the constant in Theorem <a href="#static-thm:finite-size" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="static-thm:finite-size">6</a>, then, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\label{static-eq:pressure-gap-probability} \mathbb E\frac{Z_{d,n}(D)}{Z_{n,\beta}^d} \le \exp(-n\eta/4)+\exp(-c_{\beta,d}n\eta^2).$$ The constants are uniform over $D$ and over $n$-dependent gaps satisfying the displayed lower bound.
  - Explanation: A pressure deficit for a forbidden overlap matrix gives an exponentially small averaged Gibbs probability.
- **claim-pair-pressure** (bound, [source](#stmt-static-lem-pair-bound)): For every nonempty pair constraint with overlap $q\in[0,1]$, $$\label{static-eq:pair-pressure} F_{2,n}\!\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&q\\q&1\end{smallmatrix}\right) \leq 2P_\beta-\frac12\bigl(\Gamma(q)-q\bigr)^2.$$ Consequently, for every fixed $\tau>0$, the averaged Gibbs probability of pairs satisfying $\bigl\|\Gamma(\|R(x,y)\|)-\|R(x,y)\|\bigr\|>\tau$ is at most $C\exp(-cn)$ for all large $n$.
  - Explanation: The two-replica constrained pressure is penalized by the squared distance between the scalar second moment and the prescribed overlap, so pair overlaps off the fixed-point set are exponentially rare.
- **claim-absolute-locking** (theorem, [source](#stmt-static-prop-locking)): For every fixed $h\in(0,1)$ there is $b>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\label{static-eq:locking-probability} \mathbb E\pi^{\otimes3}\!\left\{ \begin{gathered} \|R(x,y)\|\ge h,\qquad \|R(v,x)\|,\|R(v,y)\|\le b,\\ \bigl\|\|R(v,x)\|-\|R(v,y)\|\bigr\|>2n^{-1/100} \end{gathered}\right\}\le \exp(-n^{9/10}).$$ The same conclusion holds after decreasing $b$.
  - Explanation: The absolute-overlap version of locking, obtained from the signed version by spin reversal.
- **claim-energy-upper-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-static-lem-coverage-energy)): There are constants $e_1\in(0,\beta/2)$ and $c_E>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\label{static-eq:coverage-energy-good} \mathbb P_J\!\left(\pi^J\{H^J>ne_1\}>e^{-c_E n}\right) \le e^{-c_E n}.$$
  - Explanation: With overwhelming disorder probability, the Gibbs mass of high-energy configurations is exponentially small.
- **claim-disorder-rotation** (bound, [source](#stmt-static-lem-coverage-rotation)): Let $J,G$ be independent standard Gaussian vectors and, for $\theta\in(0,\pi/2)$, set $J_\theta=\cos\theta J+\sin\theta G$. Then for every $\lambda\in\mathbb R$, $$\label{static-eq:coverage-rotation-mgf} \mathbb E\exp\!\left\{\lambda(\log Z^{J_\theta}-\log Z^J)\right\} \le\exp\!\left\{\frac{\lambda^2\theta^2\beta^2(n-1)}4\right\}.$$ In particular, for every $u>0$, $$\label{static-eq:coverage-rotation-tail} \mathbb P\!\left(\log Z^{J_\theta}-\log Z^J\ge u\right) \le\exp\!\left\{-\frac{u^2}{\theta^2\beta^2(n-1)}\right\}.$$
  - Explanation: Moment-generating and tail bounds for the change of the log partition function under a Gaussian rotation of the disorder.
- **claim-stretched-coverage** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-coverage)): There exists $q_*\in(0,1)$, depending only on the fixed inverse temperature $\beta>1$, with the following property. For each fixed $D_1>0$, there exists a fixed $D_3=D_3(\beta,D_1)>0$ and a sequence of events $\mathcal C_n(D_1)$, measurable with respect to the disorder, such that $\mathbb P_J(\mathcal C_n(D_1))\longrightarrow1$ and, on $\mathcal C_n(D_1)$, $$\pi^J\!\left( \left\{x\in S: \pi^J\{z\in S:\|R(x,z)\|\ge q_*\}<e^{-D_3L} \right\}\right) <e^{-D_1L} \qquad\text{for every }S\subset\{-1,1\}^n. \label{eq:coverage}$$
  - Explanation: On the stretched-exponential scale, outside a set of tiny Gibbs mass every target in any subset has a reference neighbourhood of sufficient Gibbs mass inside that subset.
- **claim-narrow-annulus-locking** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-narrow-locking)): Suppose that $$\label{eq:narrow-identity} \Gamma(s)=s\qquad\text{for }t_0/2<s<3t_0.$$ For three independent Gibbs configurations $v,x,y$, conditionally on the disorder, define $$q=R(x,y),\qquad r=\frac{R(v,x)+R(v,y)}2,\qquad \delta=\frac{R(v,x)-R(v,y)}2.$$ Then, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\label{eq:narrow-locking-probability} \mathbb E\pi^{\otimes3}\left\{ \begin{array}{c} t_0/2<r<3t_0,\quad \mu([r-h,r+h])\le\rho^4,\\ q\ge r+\rho,\quad \varepsilon\le\|\delta\|\le2\varepsilon \end{array}\right\} \le \exp(-n^{4/5}).$$
  - Explanation: When the first overlap exceeds the level of the two smaller ones by a shrinking amount, an asymmetry of those two overlaps in a narrow window has exponentially small probability.
- **claim-polynomial-coverage** (theorem, [source](#stmt-poly-prop-coverage)): There exists $q_*=q_*(\beta)\in(0,1)$ such that, for every fixed $D_1>0$, there exists a fixed $D_2>0$ for which $$\label{poly-eq:coverage-uniform} \mathbb P_J\!\left( \text{for every }S\subset\{-1,1\}^n\text{ with }\pi^J(S)\ge n^{-D_1},\quad (\pi_S^J)^{\otimes2}\{\|R\|\ge q_*\}\ge n^{-D_2} \right)\longrightarrow1.$$ The threshold $q_*$ does not depend on $D_1$.
  - Explanation: Every disorder-selected set of polynomial Gibbs mass has polynomially large conditional mass of pairs with large absolute overlap, with a threshold fixed before the mass exponent.
- **claim-coverage-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-poly-lem-coverage-gain)): Fix $M>0$, $q\in(0,1)$, and $D\ge\beta^2M/2+2$. For all sufficiently large $n$, set $t=M\log n/n$. If $\nu$ is any deterministic probability measure on $\{-1,1\}^n$ satisfying $$\nu^{\otimes2}\{\|R\|\ge q\}\le4n^{-D},$$ and $G$ is standard Gaussian, define $W=\nu(e^{\beta\sqrt t H^G})$. There is a constant $C_\beta$, independent of $M,q,D,n,\nu$, such that $$\label{poly-eq:coverage-gain} \mathbb P_G\!\left( \log W\ge\frac{\beta^2t(n-1)}4 -\beta^2ntq-C_\beta\sqrt{nt}-\log2 \right)\ge\frac12.$$
  - Explanation: For a measure with few overlapping pairs, an independent Gaussian perturbation increases the restricted log partition sum by a quantifiable amount with probability at least one half.
- **claim-coverage-cells** (theorem, [source](#stmt-poly-cor-coverage-cells)): Let $q_*$ be as in Proposition <a href="#poly-prop:coverage" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="poly-prop:coverage">20</a>. For every fixed $D_1>0$, there exists a fixed $D_3>0$ such that, with probability tending to one over $J$, every $S\subset\{-1,1\}^n$ satisfies $$\label{poly-eq:coverage-cells} \pi^J\!\left( \left\{x\in S: \pi^J\{b\in S:\|R(x,b)\|\ge q_*\}<n^{-D_3} \right\}\right)<n^{-D_1}.$$
  - Explanation: Within any subset, the Gibbs mass of targets lacking polynomial mass of overlapping references in that subset is small.
- **claim-polynomial-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fixed-thm-polynomial)): For every fixed $\beta>1$ and every fixed $a>0$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb P_{J,\,x_0\sim\pi^J} \left\{d^J(x_0,n^a)>\frac14\right\}&\longrightarrow1, \label{fixed-eq:continuous-conclusion}\\ \mathbb P_{J,\,x_0\sim\pi^J} \left\{d_{\mathrm{disc}}^J(x_0,\lfloor n^a\rfloor) >\frac14\right\} &\longrightarrow1. \label{fixed-eq:discrete-conclusion} \end{aligned}$$ Consequently both $\mathbb P\{T^J(x_0)>n^a\}$ and $\mathbb P\{T_{\mathrm{disc}}^J(x_0)>n^a\}$ tend to one under the same joint law. Each assertion is equivalently a statement that the Gibbs mass of the indicated initial states tends to one in probability over $J$.
  - Explanation: A second proof, with a fixed number of crossing levels, that the point distance to equilibrium stays above the threshold at every fixed polynomial time scale.
- **claim-bank-coverage** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fixed-lem-bank-coverage)): With probability $1-o(1)$, for every $1\le j\le p$, every tuple $\boldsymbol v$ from banks $1,\ldots,j-1$, and every $0\le k\le m$, $$x_k\in S_j(\boldsymbol v) \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \text{there is }\ell\le M_j\text{ such that } V_{j,\ell}\in S_j(\boldsymbol v),\quad \|R(V_{j,\ell},x_k)\|\ge q_*.$$ This event depends only on $J$, the chain and the bank lists, and is independent of the thresholds.
  - Explanation: The polynomial reference banks simultaneously cover every path point in every cell.
- **claim-bank-locking** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fixed-lem-bank-locking)): With probability $1-o(1)$, for every $i<j\le p$, every entry $v$ of bank $i$, every entry $w$ of bank $j$, and every $0\le k\le m$, $$\label{fixed-eq:all-bank-locking} \begin{gathered} \|R(x_k,w)\|\ge t_j/2,\qquad \|R(v,x_k)\|,\ \|R(v,w)\|\le b_j \\ \Longrightarrow\qquad \bigl\|\|R(v,x_k)\|-\|R(v,w)\|\bigr\|\le2\delta_n. \end{gathered}$$
  - Explanation: Locking holds simultaneously for all bank entries and all path indices.
- **claim-nested-crossings** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fixed-lem-nested-crossings)): For all sufficiently large $n$, on $\mathcal G_n\cap\mathcal S_n$ every reference selection and every crossing succeeds. For every $1\le j\le p$, $$\label{fixed-eq:nested-shells} \|D_{v_j}(\tau_j)-r_j\|\le2/n,\qquad \|D_{v_i}(\tau_j)-r_i\|\le6j\delta_n \quad(1\le i\le j).$$
  - Explanation: On the good event together with a sign passage, every selection and crossing succeeds and all reference depths sit near their thresholds.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-static-prop-matrix-bound",
        "exact_quote": "For every feasible $D$ and every path and multiplier as above, $$\\label{static-eq:matrix-bound}\n F_{d,n}(D)\\le\n \\Psi(Q,x,\\lambda)-\\lambda:D\n +\\frac{\\beta^2}{4}\n       \\left(|D-Q_{\\rm end}|^2-\\int x\\,\\,\\mathrm d|Q|^2\\right).$$ For fixed $D$ and $\\lambda$, the bound passes to any sequence of finite paths for which $Q_{\\rm end}$, $\\Psi(Q,x,\\lambda)$, and the displayed path integral converge.",

        "source_id": "PM-227.6"

          "source_id": "PM-227.6"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-static-lem-scalar-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "The step approximation defining $\\Phi_\\alpha$ is independent of the approximating sequence. The functions $\\Phi_\\alpha,u,\\chi$ are continuous on $[0,1]\\times\\mathbb R$, with $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-derivative-bounds}\n |u|\\le1,\\qquad 0<\\chi\\le1,\\qquad\n |\\chi(s,z)-\\chi(s,z')|\\le C_\\beta|z-z'|.$$ The function $\\Phi_\\alpha(s,\\cdot)$ is even, so $u(s,0)=0$. For two distribution functions $\\alpha,\\widetilde\\alpha$, $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-stability}\n \\sup_{s,z}\\bigl(\n |\\Phi_\\alpha-\\Phi_{\\widetilde\\alpha}|\n +|u_\\alpha-u_{\\widetilde\\alpha}|\n +|\\chi_\\alpha-\\chi_{\\widetilde\\alpha}|\n \\bigr)\n \\le C_\\beta\\int_0^1|\\alpha(s)-\\widetilde\\alpha(s)|\\,\\mathrm ds.$$ These functions are also continuous in $\\beta>0$, uniformly in $(s,z)$ for their differences, locally in $\\beta$.*\n\n\n\n\n\n\n*The stochastic differential equation $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-diffusion}\n \\,\\mathrm dX_s=\\beta\\,\\mathrm dB_s+\\beta^2\\alpha(s)u(s,X_s)\\,\\mathrm ds,\n \\qquad X_0=0,$$ has a unique strong solution. For a step distribution function, its transitions across the constant-mass intervals are the normalized tilted Gaussian kernels of the scalar recursion. Its bounded martingale $U_s=u(s,X_s)$ satisfies $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-martingale}\n U_t-U_s=\\beta\\int_s^t\\chi(v,X_v)\\,\\mathrm dB_v.$$ Moreover, $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation}\n \\mathbb E\\chi(t,X_t)-\\mathbb E\\chi(s,X_s)\n =-\\beta^2\\int_s^t\\alpha(v)\\mathbb E\\chi(v,X_v)^2\\,\\mathrm dv.$$ For $0\\le s<t\\le1$, the conditional form is $$\\label{eq:scalar-evolution}\n \\mathbb E[\\chi(t,X_t)\\mid X_s]\n =\\chi(s,X_s)-\\beta^2\\int_s^t\\alpha(v)\n                   \\mathbb E[\\chi(v,X_v)^2\\mid X_s]\\,\\mathrm dv.$$ Consequently $\\Gamma(s):=\\mathbb Eu(s,X_s)^2$ is continuously differentiable, with one-sided derivatives at the endpoints, and $$\\label{static-eq:gamma-derivative}\n \\Gamma(0)=0,\\qquad\n \\Gamma'(s)=\\beta^2\\mathbb E\\chi(s,X_s)^2.$$ The well-posedness statement and the evolution identities <a href=\"#static-eq:scalar-martingale\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"static-eq:scalar-martingale\">[static-eq:scalar-martingale]</a>–<a href=\"#static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation\">[static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation]</a> also hold for a diffusion started at any deterministic time and spatial value.",
        "source_id": "PM-227.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-static-lem-scalar-regularity",
          "exact_quote": "The step approximation defining $\\Phi_\\alpha$ is independent of the approximating sequence. For two distribution functions $\\alpha,\\widetilde\\alpha$, $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-stability}\n \\sup_{s,z}\\bigl(\n |\\Phi_\\alpha-\\Phi_{\\widetilde\\alpha}|\n +|u_\\alpha-u_{\\widetilde\\alpha}|\n +|\\chi_\\alpha-\\chi_{\\widetilde\\alpha}|\n \\bigr)\n \\le C_\\beta\\int_0^1|\\alpha(s)-\\widetilde\\alpha(s)|\\,\\mathrm ds.$$ These functions are also continuous in $\\beta>0$, uniformly in $(s,z)$ for their differences, locally in $\\beta$.*\n\n\n\n\n\n\n*The stochastic differential equation $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-diffusion}\n \\,\\mathrm dX_s=\\beta\\,\\mathrm dB_s+\\beta^2\\alpha(s)u(s,X_s)\\,\\mathrm ds,\n \\qquad X_0=0,$$ has a unique strong solution. For a step distribution function, its transitions across the constant-mass intervals are the normalized tilted Gaussian kernels of the scalar recursion. Its bounded martingale $U_s=u(s,X_s)$ satisfies $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-martingale}\n U_t-U_s=\\beta\\int_s^t\\chi(v,X_v)\\,\\mathrm dB_v.$$ Moreover, $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation}\n \\mathbb E\\chi(t,X_t)-\\mathbb E\\chi(s,X_s)\n =-\\beta^2\\int_s^t\\alpha(v)\\mathbb E\\chi(v,X_v)^2\\,\\mathrm dv.$$ For $0\\le s<t\\le1$, the conditional form is $$\\label{eq:scalar-evolution}\n \\mathbb E[\\chi(t,X_t)\\mid X_s]\n =\\chi(s,X_s)-\\beta^2\\int_s^t\\alpha(v)\n                   \\mathbb E[\\chi(v,X_v)^2\\mid X_s]\\,\\mathrm dv.$$ Consequently $\\Gamma(s):=\\mathbb Eu(s,X_s)^2$ is continuously differentiable, with one-sided derivatives at the endpoints, and $$\\label{static-eq:gamma-derivative}\n \\Gamma(0)=0,\\qquad\n \\Gamma'(s)=\\beta^2\\mathbb E\\chi(s,X_s)^2.$$ The well-posedness statement and the evolution identities <a href=\"#static-eq:scalar-martingale\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"static-eq:scalar-martingale\">[static-eq:scalar-martingale]</a>–<a href=\"#static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation\">[static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation]</a> also hold for a diffusion started at any deterministic time and spatial value.",

      "exact_statement": "The step approximation defining $\\Phi_\\alpha$ is independent of the approximating sequence. For two distribution functions $\\alpha,\\widetilde\\alpha$, $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-stability}\n \\sup_{s,z}\\bigl(\n |\\Phi_\\alpha-\\Phi_{\\widetilde\\alpha}|\n +|u_\\alpha-u_{\\widetilde\\alpha}|\n +|\\chi_\\alpha-\\chi_{\\widetilde\\alpha}|\n \\bigr)\n \\le C_\\beta\\int_0^1|\\alpha(s)-\\widetilde\\alpha(s)|\\,\\mathrm ds.$$ These functions are also continuous in $\\beta>0$, uniformly in $(s,z)$ for their differences, locally in $\\beta$.*\n\n\n\n\n\n\n*The stochastic differential equation $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-diffusion}\n \\,\\mathrm dX_s=\\beta\\,\\mathrm dB_s+\\beta^2\\alpha(s)u(s,X_s)\\,\\mathrm ds,\n \\qquad X_0=0,$$ has a unique strong solution. For a step distribution function, its transitions across the constant-mass intervals are the normalized tilted Gaussian kernels of the scalar recursion. Its bounded martingale $U_s=u(s,X_s)$ satisfies $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-martingale}\n U_t-U_s=\\beta\\int_s^t\\chi(v,X_v)\\,\\mathrm dB_v.$$ Moreover, $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation}\n \\mathbb E\\chi(t,X_t)-\\mathbb E\\chi(s,X_s)\n =-\\beta^2\\int_s^t\\alpha(v)\\mathbb E\\chi(v,X_v)^2\\,\\mathrm dv.$$ For $0\\le s<t\\le1$, the conditional form is $$\\label{eq:scalar-evolution}\n \\mathbb E[\\chi(t,X_t)\\mid X_s]\n =\\chi(s,X_s)-\\beta^2\\int_s^t\\alpha(v)\n                   \\mathbb E[\\chi(v,X_v)^2\\mid X_s]\\,\\mathrm dv.$$ Consequently $\\Gamma(s):=\\mathbb Eu(s,X_s)^2$ is continuously differentiable, with one-sided derivatives at the endpoints, and $$\\label{static-eq:gamma-derivative}\n \\Gamma(0)=0,\\qquad\n \\Gamma'(s)=\\beta^2\\mathbb E\\chi(s,X_s)^2.$$ The well-posedness statement and the evolution identities <a href=\"#static-eq:scalar-martingale\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"static-eq:scalar-martingale\">[static-eq:scalar-martingale]</a>–<a href=\"#static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation\">[static-eq:scalar-chi-expectation]</a> also hold for a diffusion started at any deterministic time and spatial value.",
      "explanation": "Well-posedness, derivative bounds, stability and the scalar diffusion evolution identities for the Parisi equation with a general distribution function.",

      "id": "claim-scalar-regularity",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-static-prop-scalar-minimizer",
        "exact_quote": "The scalar functional has a minimizer $\\mu$. For every such minimizer, with its associated scalar process, the following assertions hold:*\n\n1.  *$0\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu$, and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu\\subset[0,1-\\varepsilon_0]$ for some $\\varepsilon_0>0$.*\n\n2.  *At every $q\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu\\cap(0,1)$, $\\Gamma(q)=q$ and $\\Gamma'(q)\\le1$. At $q=0$, $\\Gamma(0)=0$ and $\\Gamma'(0)\\le1$.*\n\n\n\n\n\n3.  *Define $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-A-d-definitions}\n       d_*(s)=1-\\int_{(s,1]}t\\,\\mu(\\,\\mathrm dt),\\qquad\n       A(s)=\\int_s^1\\alpha(t)\\,\\mathrm dt=d_*(s)-s\\alpha(s).$$ Then, at every $s\\in[0,1]$, $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-stationarity-identity}\n       \\mathbb E\\bigl[\\chi(s,X_s)+\\alpha(s)u(s,X_s)^2\\bigr]=d_*(s).$$ Equivalently, since $d_*(s)=A(s)+s\\alpha(s)$, $$\\label{eq:scalar-stationarity}\n       \\mathbb E\\chi(s,X_s)=A(s)+\\alpha(s)(s-\\Gamma(s))\n           \\qquad(0\\le s\\le1).$$ In particular $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-susceptibility}\n       \\chi(0,0)=A(0),\\qquad \\beta A(0)\\le1.$$ Furthermore $\\alpha(s)>0$ for every $s\\in(0,1]$, $A(h)<A(0)$ for every $h\\in(0,1]$, and $d_*(0+)=A(0)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-227.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-static-prop-scalar-minimizer",
          "exact_quote": "The scalar functional has a minimizer $\\mu$. For every such minimizer, with its associated scalar process, the following assertions hold:*\n\n1.  *$0\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu$, and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu\\subset[0,1-\\varepsilon_0]$ for some $\\varepsilon_0>0$.*\n\n2.  *At every $q\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu\\cap(0,1)$, $\\Gamma(q)=q$ and $\\Gamma'(q)\\le1$. At $q=0$, $\\Gamma(0)=0$ and $\\Gamma'(0)\\le1$.*\n\n\n\n\n\n3.  *Define $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-A-d-definitions}\n       d_*(s)=1-\\int_{(s,1]}t\\,\\mu(\\,\\mathrm dt),\\qquad\n       A(s)=\\int_s^1\\alpha(t)\\,\\mathrm dt=d_*(s)-s\\alpha(s).$$ Then, at every $s\\in[0,1]$, $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-stationarity-identity}\n       \\mathbb E\\bigl[\\chi(s,X_s)+\\alpha(s)u(s,X_s)^2\\bigr]=d_*(s).$$ Equivalently, since $d_*(s)=A(s)+s\\alpha(s)$, $$\\label{eq:scalar-stationarity}\n       \\mathbb E\\chi(s,X_s)=A(s)+\\alpha(s)(s-\\Gamma(s))\n           \\qquad(0\\le s\\le1).$$ In particular $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-susceptibility}\n       \\chi(0,0)=A(0),\\qquad \\beta A(0)\\le1.$$ Furthermore $\\alpha(s)>0$ for every $s\\in(0,1]$, $A(h)<A(0)$ for every $h\\in(0,1]$, and $d_*(0+)=A(0)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-227.6"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The scalar functional has a minimizer $\\mu$. For every such minimizer, with its associated scalar process, the following assertions hold:*\n\n1.  *$0\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu$, and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu\\subset[0,1-\\varepsilon_0]$ for some $\\varepsilon_0>0$.*\n\n2.  *At every $q\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu\\cap(0,1)$, $\\Gamma(q)=q$ and $\\Gamma'(q)\\le1$. At $q=0$, $\\Gamma(0)=0$ and $\\Gamma'(0)\\le1$.*\n\n\n\n\n\n3.  *Define $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-A-d-definitions}\n       d_*(s)=1-\\int_{(s,1]}t\\,\\mu(\\,\\mathrm dt),\\qquad\n       A(s)=\\int_s^1\\alpha(t)\\,\\mathrm dt=d_*(s)-s\\alpha(s).$$ Then, at every $s\\in[0,1]$, $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-stationarity-identity}\n       \\mathbb E\\bigl[\\chi(s,X_s)+\\alpha(s)u(s,X_s)^2\\bigr]=d_*(s).$$ Equivalently, since $d_*(s)=A(s)+s\\alpha(s)$, $$\\label{eq:scalar-stationarity}\n       \\mathbb E\\chi(s,X_s)=A(s)+\\alpha(s)(s-\\Gamma(s))\n           \\qquad(0\\le s\\le1).$$ In particular $$\\label{static-eq:scalar-susceptibility}\n       \\chi(0,0)=A(0),\\qquad \\beta A(0)\\le1.$$ Furthermore $\\alpha(s)>0$ for every $s\\in(0,1]$, $A(h)<A(0)$ for every $h\\in(0,1]$, and $d_*(0+)=A(0)$.",
      "explanation": "A minimizer of the scalar functional exists; its support contains zero and stays below one, and it satisfies stationarity and susceptibility identities.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-static-lem-energy-gap",
        "exact_quote": "For each fixed $\\beta>1$, there are $e_0=e_0(\\beta)<\\beta/2$ and $N=N(\\beta)$ such that $$\\label{static-eq:energy-gap}\n p_n'(\\beta)=\\frac1n\\mathbb E\\pi_{n,\\beta}^J(H_n^J)\\le e_0\n \\qquad(n\\ge N).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-227.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-static-lem-energy-gap",
          "exact_quote": "For each fixed $\\beta>1$, there are $e_0=e_0(\\beta)<\\beta/2$ and $N=N(\\beta)$ such that $$\\label{static-eq:energy-gap}\n p_n'(\\beta)=\\frac1n\\mathbb E\\pi_{n,\\beta}^J(H_n^J)\\le e_0\n \\qquad(n\\ge N).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-227.6"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For each fixed $\\beta>1$, there are $e_0=e_0(\\beta)<\\beta/2$ and $N=N(\\beta)$ such that $$\\label{static-eq:energy-gap}\n p_n'(\\beta)=\\frac1n\\mathbb E\\pi_{n,\\beta}^J(H_n^J)\\le e_0\n \\qquad(n\\ge N).$$",

      "hypotheses": [

        "anchor": "stmt-static-lem-pair-bound",
        "exact_quote": "For every nonempty pair constraint with overlap $q\\in[0,1]$, $$\\label{static-eq:pair-pressure}\n F_{2,n}\\!\\left(\\begin{smallmatrix}1&q\\\\q&1\\end{smallmatrix}\\right)\n \\leq 2P_\\beta-\\frac12\\bigl(\\Gamma(q)-q\\bigr)^2.$$ Consequently, for every fixed $\\tau>0$, the averaged Gibbs probability of pairs satisfying $\\bigl|\\Gamma(|R(x,y)|)-|R(x,y)|\\bigr|>\\tau$ is at most $C\\exp(-cn)$ for all large $n$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-static-lem-coverage-rotation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $J,G$ be independent standard Gaussian vectors and, for $\\theta\\in(0,\\pi/2)$, set $J_\\theta=\\cos\\theta J+\\sin\\theta G$. Then for every $\\lambda\\in\\mathbb R$, $$\\label{static-eq:coverage-rotation-mgf}\n \\mathbb E\\exp\\!\\left\\{\\lambda(\\log Z^{J_\\theta}-\\log Z^J)\\right\\}\n \\le\\exp\\!\\left\\{\\frac{\\lambda^2\\theta^2\\beta^2(n-1)}4\\right\\}.$$ In particular, for every $u>0$, $$\\label{static-eq:coverage-rotation-tail}\n \\mathbb P\\!\\left(\\log Z^{J_\\theta}-\\log Z^J\\ge u\\right)\n \\le\\exp\\!\\left\\{-\\frac{u^2}{\\theta^2\\beta^2(n-1)}\\right\\}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-227.6"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-227.6"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-coverage",
        "exact_quote": "There exists $q_*\\in(0,1)$, depending only on the fixed inverse temperature $\\beta>1$, with the following property. For each fixed $D_1>0$, there exists a fixed $D_3=D_3(\\beta,D_1)>0$ and a sequence of events $\\mathcal C_n(D_1)$, measurable with respect to the disorder, such that $\\mathbb P_J(\\mathcal C_n(D_1))\\longrightarrow1$ and, on $\\mathcal C_n(D_1)$, $$\\pi^J\\!\\left(\n   \\left\\{x\\in S:\n     \\pi^J\\{z\\in S:|R(x,z)|\\ge q_*\\}<e^{-D_3L}\n   \\right\\}\\right)\n <e^{-D_1L}\n \\qquad\\text{for every }S\\subset\\{-1,1\\}^n.\n \\label{eq:coverage}$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-narrow-locking",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $$\\label{eq:narrow-identity}\n \\Gamma(s)=s\\qquad\\text{for }t_0/2<s<3t_0.$$ For three independent Gibbs configurations $v,x,y$, conditionally on the disorder, define $$q=R(x,y),\\qquad\n r=\\frac{R(v,x)+R(v,y)}2,\\qquad\n \\delta=\\frac{R(v,x)-R(v,y)}2.$$ Then, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\\label{eq:narrow-locking-probability}\n \\mathbb E\\pi^{\\otimes3}\\left\\{\n \\begin{array}{c}\n t_0/2<r<3t_0,\\quad \\mu([r-h,r+h])\\le\\rho^4,\\\\\n q\\ge r+\\rho,\\quad \\varepsilon\\le|\\delta|\\le2\\varepsilon\n \\end{array}\\right\\}\n \\le \\exp(-n^{4/5}).$$",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **zero-field SK model** ([source](#eq:model)): For an integer $n\ge2$, let $\Sigma_n=\{-1,1\}^n$, and let $(J_{ij})_{1\le i<j\le n}$ be independent standard Gaussian variables. At inverse temperature $\beta>0$, the zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model has Hamiltonian and Gibbs measure $$\label{eq:model} H^J(x)=H_n^J(x)=\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{i<j}J_{ij}x_ix_j, \qquad \pi^J(x)=\pi_{n,\beta}^J(x) =\frac{e^{\beta H^J(x)}}{Z_{n,\beta}^J}, \qquad x\in\Sigma_n,$$ where $Z^J=Z_{n,\beta}^J=\sum_{x\in\Sigma_n}e^{\beta H^J(x)}$. For $x,y\in\Sigma_n$, their overlap is $$R(x,y)=\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n x_i y_i.$$ The measure $\pi^J$ is invariant under the global spin reversal $x\mapsto-x$. We fix $\beta>1$ before sending $n$ to infinity.
- **point mixing times** ([source](#eq:point-mixing-time)): $$\begin{aligned} d^J(x,t)&=\lVert P_t^J(x,\cdot)-\pi^J\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}, &T^J(x)&=\inf\{t\ge0:d^J(x,t)\le1/4\}, \label{eq:point-mixing-time}\\ d_{\mathrm{disc}}^J(x,k)&= \lVert (K^J)^k(x,\cdot)-\pi^J\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}, &T_{\mathrm{disc}}^J(x)&= \min\{k\ge0:d_{\mathrm{disc}}^J(x,k)\le1/4\}. \label{eq:point-discrete-mixing-time} \end{aligned}$$ Here $k$ is an integer, and neither mixing time takes a supremum over the initial state.
- **hierarchy recursion** ([source](#static-eq:hierarchy-recursion)): We next define the hierarchy and its probability law. Besides any quenched root randomness, take independent Gaussian fields $g_0,\ldots,g_k$. The field $g_0$ belongs to the quenched root. Choose masses $$0=x_{-1}\le x_0\le\cdots\le x_{k-1}\le x_k=1, \qquad w_l=x_l-x_{l-1},$$ and a terminal log partition function $F_k$ on a finite configuration space. Integrate the fields backwards by setting, for $1\le j\le k$, $$\label{static-eq:hierarchy-recursion} F_{j-1}=\frac1{x_{j-1}}\log\mathbb E_j e^{x_{j-1}F_j}.$$ Here $\mathbb E_j$ integrates the field $g_j$. At a zero mass the right-hand side is interpreted as $\mathbb E_jF_j$. After these recursive integrations, the hierarchy pressure is $\mathbb EF_0$, with expectation also over all quenched randomness.
- **hierarchy tilt law** ([source](#static-eq:hierarchy-tilt)): The recursion also specifies a probability law on fields and terminal configurations. Conditional on the prefix through level $j-1$, multiply the original Gaussian law of $g_j$ by the density $$\label{static-eq:hierarchy-tilt} W_j=\exp\{x_{j-1}(F_j-F_{j-1})\}.$$ Equation <a href="#static-eq:hierarchy-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="static-eq:hierarchy-recursion">[static-eq:hierarchy-recursion]</a> normalizes this density. After sampling level $k$, draw a configuration from the terminal Gibbs law. For a pair of paths indexed by a level $l$, sample their root and fields through $l$ once, and then sample the two continuations independently conditional on this prefix. We denote expectation under that pair law by $\mathbb E_l^{(2)}$. At $l=k$ the only independent choices are the terminal thermal draws. More generally, a finite tree is sampled by drawing each shared prefix once and making its descendants conditionally independent. In particular, every branch has the single-path marginal law. This convention fixes the shared randomness in all subsequent integration-by-parts calculations.
- **constrained pressure** ([source](#static-eq:constrained-pressure)): We now apply the differentiation identity to a prescribed overlap constraint. For $d$ configurations $\sigma^1,\ldots,\sigma^d$, write $D_{ab}=R(\sigma^a,\sigma^b)$ for their Gram matrix. If $D$ is feasible, meaning that at least one such tuple realizes it, define $$\label{static-eq:constrained-pressure} F_{d,n}(D)=\frac1n\mathbb E\log \sum_{\substack{\sigma^1,\ldots,\sigma^d\in\{-1,1\}^n\\ R(\sigma^a,\sigma^b)=D_{ab}\ \forall a,b}} \exp\left\{\beta\sum_{a=1}^dH_n(\sigma^a)\right\}.$$ The sum is unnormalized. We use the Frobenius notation $U:V=\sum_{a,b}U_{ab}V_{ab}$ and $\|U\|^2=U:U$. To compare this pressure with a single-site recursion, choose a symmetric matrix path $$Q_{-1}=0,\qquad Q_j-Q_{j-1}\succeq0\quad(0\le j\le k), \qquad Q_k=Q_{\rm end}.$$ Within these conditions, the endpoint is free: it may differ from $D$ and may have nonunit diagonal. Increments of zero covariance are allowed. If the first nonzero increment should carry positive mass, insert a quenched level $Q_0=0$; the first genuine increment is then integrated at mass $x_0$. At a single site, take independent $d$-dimensional Gaussian fields of covariance $\beta^2(Q_j-Q_{j-1})$. For a symmetric multiplier $\lambda$, define $\Psi(Q,x,\lambda)$ as the expected hierarchy value with terminal function $$\log\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}^d} \exp\left\{\left(\sum_{j=0}^kg_j\right)\cdot s +s^{\mathsf T}\lambda s\right\}.$$ For the deterministic correction to the pressure, set $$\label{static-eq:matrix-path-integral} \int x\,\,\mathrm d\|Q\|^2 =\sum_{j=0}^kx_{j-1}\bigl(\|Q_j\|^2-\|Q_{j-1}\|^2\bigr).$$ The initial quenched increment therefore carries mass zero in this integral. The next bound keeps the cost of the free endpoint explicit.
- **scalar Parisi functional** ([source](#static-eq:scalar-functional)): $$\label{static-eq:scalar-functional} \mathcal P_\beta(\mu)=\Phi_\alpha(0,0) -\frac{\beta^2}{2}\int_0^1s\alpha(s)\,\mathrm ds, \qquad P_\beta=\inf_\mu\mathcal P_\beta(\mu).$$ The infimum is attained by compactness and continuity, as proved in Proposition <a href="#static-prop:scalar-minimizer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="static-prop:scalar-minimizer">10</a>. These scalar approximation and compactness arguments are independent of the pressure comparison; the lower comparison below will use only finitely supported trial measures. Apply Proposition <a href="#static-prop:matrix-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="static-prop:matrix-bound">5</a> with $d=1$, $D=Q_{\rm end}=1$, and $\lambda=0$. First for step measures and then by approximation, this gives the upper comparison $$\label{static-eq:scalar-upper-bound} p_n(\beta):=\frac1n\mathbb E\log Z_{n,\beta}\le P_\beta.$$
- **hierarchy means and overlap means** ([source](#static-eq:hierarchy-means)): $$\label{static-eq:hierarchy-means} m_j=\mathbb E[\sigma\mid\mathcal A_j],\qquad M_j=\frac{\\|m_j\\|^2}{n},\qquad r_j=\mathbb EM_j.$$ The conditional means $m_j$ form a martingale. Consequently $0\le r_0\le\cdots\le r_k\le1$. They can also be obtained by differentiating $F_j$ with respect to an additional deterministic field coupled linearly to the spins. We begin by controlling the Gaussian field energy under the same path law.
- **cell of a reference tuple** ([source](#fixed-eq:cell)): For such a tuple $\boldsymbol v=(v_1,\ldots,v_{j-1})$, define its cell by $$\label{fixed-eq:cell} S_j(\boldsymbol v) =\{z\in\{-1,1\}^n: \|R(v_i,z)\|\le3t_i\text{ for every }i<j\}.$$ In particular $S_1=\{-1,1\}^n$. The cell restricts the small overlaps with the earlier references, while coverage will supply a large overlap with the current target inside that cell.
- **block parameters** ([source](#eq:dyn-block-parameters)): $$\label{eq:dyn-block-parameters} H_0=\lceil 2/b^2\rceil,\qquad B=H_0+2,\qquad p=B\left\lfloor\frac{t_0}{5B\rho}\right\rfloor.$$ For large $n$, $p\ge B$. Divide the $p$ levels into consecutive blocks of $B$ levels each. At the first level of every block after the first, we *reset* the selection cell to the whole space. Older retained references are kept unless they meet the conflict rule specified below. Independently of the disorder, banks and path, draw independent thresholds $r_i$, uniform on $$\label{eq:dyn-threshold-intervals} I_i=[t_0+4(i-1)\rho,\ t_0+(4(i-1)+1)\rho].$$ These intervals lie in $[t_0,2t_0]$, and $$\label{eq:dyn-threshold-separation} r_j-r_i\geq3\rho\qquad(i<j).$$ Fubini’s theorem gives $$\mathbb E\big[\mu([r_i-2h,r_i+2h])\big]\leq\frac{4h}{\rho}.$$ Consequently Markov’s inequality and a union bound show that the event $$\label{eq:dyn-threshold-good} \mathcal T=\{\mu([r_i-2h,r_i+2h])\leq\rho^4 \text{ for every }1\leq i\leq p\}$$ satisfies $$\label{eq:dyn-threshold-good-prob} \mathbb P(\mathcal T^c)\leq\frac{4ph}{\rho^5}=O(\rho^2).$$ The Fubini calculation includes atoms; no regularity of the distribution of the minimizing measure is imposed here.
- **conflict rule** ([source](#eq:dyn-conflict)): $$\label{eq:dyn-conflict} \|R(v_i,v_j)\|>b.$$ The remaining old indices are called compatible. Search backwards from $\tau_{j-1}$, stopping at the first point encountered with overlap at most $r_j$ with $v_j$. Equivalently, if the set is nonempty, define $$\label{eq:dyn-backward-time} \tau_j=\max\{0\leq k\leq\tau_{j-1}:R(v_j,x_k)\leq r_j\}.$$ Add $j$ to the compatible list to obtain $A_j$.
- **narrow-annulus scales** ([source](#eq:narrow-scales)): $$\label{eq:narrow-scales} h=\rho^8,\qquad \varepsilon=\rho^{20}.$$ Constants below may depend on $\beta,t_0$ and the fixed minimizing measure. They do not depend on $n$ or on the overlap parameters.
- **Frobenius notation** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For matrices, $U:V=\sum_{a,b}U_{ab}V_{ab}$ and $\|U\|^2=U:U$ denote the Frobenius inner product and squared norm.

          "exact_quote": "We next define the hierarchy and its probability law. Besides any quenched root randomness, take independent Gaussian fields $g_0,\\ldots,g_k$. At a zero mass the right-hand side is interpreted as $\\mathbb E_jF_j$.           "anchor": "static-eq:hierarchy-tilt",
          "exact_quote": "The recursion also specifies a probability law on fields and terminal configurations. After sampling level $k$, draw a configuration from the terminal Gibbs law. For a pair of paths indexed by a level $l$, sample their root and fields through $l$ once, and then sample the two continuations independently conditional on this prefix. We denote expectation under that pair law by $\\mathbb E_l^{(2)}$. At $l=k$ the only independent choices are the terminal thermal draws. More generally, a finite tree is sampled by drawing each shared prefix once and making its descendants conditionally independent. In particular, every branch has the single-path marginal law. This convention fixes the shared randomness in all subsequent integration-by-parts calculations.",

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-main-exponent | stretched-exponential exponent | = | $1/10000$ | fixed inverse temperature above one |
| q-tv-threshold | lower bound for the point total-variation distance | > | $\frac14$ | time equal to the stretched-exponential scale |
| q-finite-size-gap | gap between the scalar Parisi minimum and the pressure | ≤ | $C_\beta n^{-1/24}$ | fixed inverse temperature |
| q-field-energy-variance | variance of the normalized field energy | ≤ | $\frac1n$ | all levels |
| q-energy-gap | equilibrium energy density bound | < | $\beta/2$ | n at least a threshold depending on the temperature |
| q-locking-probability | averaged probability of an absolute-overlap locking violation | ≤ | $\exp(-n^{9/10})$ | cutoffs depending on the temperature |
| q-narrow-probability | probability of a narrow-annulus asymmetry event | ≤ | $\exp(-n^{4/5})$ | identity of the scalar second moment on a fixed interval |
| q-time-horizon | deterministic index horizon of the discrete path | = | $m=\lceil e^{2L}\rceil$ | L equal to the power of n with the main exponent |
| q-conflict-bound | maximal conflict suffix length parameter | = | $H_0=\lceil 2/b^2\rceil$ | fixed cutoff b |
| q-narrow-window | narrow-annulus window width | = | $\rho^8$ | scale parameter rho |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-gaussian-tools** [claim](#stmt-static-lem-gaussian-tools) / [proof](#proof-stmt-static-lem-gaussian-tools): Exponential martingale for concentration; diffusion with synchronous coupling for the linear variance bound. -> concentration, moment bound and linear variance bound
- **step-hierarchy-derivative** [claim](#stmt-static-lem-hierarchy-derivative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-static-lem-hierarchy-derivative): Differentiate the recursion twice, integrate by parts in the Gaussian field and telescope. -> derivative identity of the hierarchy pressure
- **step-matrix-bound** [claim](#stmt-static-prop-matrix-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-static-prop-matrix-bound): Interpolate from single-site fields to the Hamiltonian disorder and apply the derivative identity. -> upper bound on constrained pressure
- **step-field-energy** [claim](#stmt-static-lem-field-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-static-lem-field-energy): Convexity of the hierarchy values gives a strongly convex conditional density; gauge symmetry removes the conditional mean. -> variance bound for the field energy
- **step-average-overlap** [claim](#stmt-static-lem-average-overlap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-static-lem-average-overlap): Total variance split into fluctuation of the conditional mean and conditional variance, via tree integration by parts and eigenvalue bounds. -> average overlap concentration
- **step-finite-size** [claim](#stmt-static-thm-finite-size) / [proof](#proof-stmt-static-lem-average-overlap-2): Adaptive interpolation along an overlap-driven differential equation, then identification with a scalar trial measure. -> two-sided pressure comparison
- **step-scalar-regularity** [claim](#stmt-static-lem-scalar-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-static-lem-scalar-regularity): Uniform estimates over step paths, stability by diffusion representation, passage to the limit and continuity in temperature. -> regularity and evolution identities for the scalar equation
- **step-scalar-minimizer** [claim](#stmt-static-prop-scalar-minimizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-static-prop-scalar-minimizer): Compactness for existence; first variation for the support; martingale argument for the left endpoint; Stieltjes integration by parts for stationarity. -> support and stationarity identities
- **step-energy-gap** [claim](#stmt-static-lem-energy-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-static-lem-energy-gap): Temperature derivative at a fixed minimizer, then a forward secant estimate for the finite-volume pressure. -> strict equilibrium energy bound
- **step-pressure-probability** [claim](#stmt-lem-pressure-prob) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pressure-prob): Lipschitz bound for the difference of log partition functions and Gaussian concentration. -> probability bound from a pressure gap
- **step-pair-bound** [claim](#stmt-static-lem-pair-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-static-lem-pair-bound): Two-coordinate hierarchy with a terminal coupling term and a Taylor bound on the second derivative. -> pair pressure deficit
- **step-fixed-locking** [claim](#stmt-lem-fixed-locking) / [proof](#proof-stmt-static-lem-pair-bound-2): Three-replica perturbed hierarchy with strict quadratic gain, then conversion of the pressure deficit to a probability bound and a union over overlap triples. -> signed fixed-scale locking
- **step-absolute-locking** [claim](#stmt-static-prop-locking) / [proof](#proof-stmt-static-prop-locking): Reduce to the signed event by reversing a replica. -> absolute-overlap locking
- **step-energy-tail** [claim](#stmt-static-lem-coverage-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-static-lem-coverage-energy): Strongly convex posterior of the disorder given a sampled configuration, Chernoff bound, then Markov's inequality. -> upper energy tail
- **step-rotation** [claim](#stmt-static-lem-coverage-rotation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-static-lem-coverage-rotation): Rotation interpolation, Jensen's inequality and Gaussian integration. -> rotation moment and tail bounds
- **step-stretched-coverage** [claim](#stmt-prop-coverage) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-coverage): Select one violating set, trim the energy tail, use Paley–Zygmund and concentration for the Gaussian gain, compare with the rotation cost. -> stretched-exponential coverage
- **step-narrow-locking** [claim](#stmt-lem-narrow-locking) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-narrow-locking): Perturbed hierarchy with a tent on one branch and a ramp on the other, strict susceptibility loss, pressure-to-probability conversion. -> narrow-annulus locking
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:dynamics): Reduce to a sign passage, build banks and nested backward crossings with block resets, and bound the probability by predictable thresholds. -> stretched-exponential mixing obstruction
- **step-superpolynomial** [claim](#stmt-cor-superpolynomial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-superpolynomial): Compare polynomial time with the stretched-exponential time and use contraction of total variation. -> superpolynomial mixing time
- **step-coverage-gain** [claim](#stmt-poly-lem-coverage-gain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-poly-lem-coverage-gain): Second moment of the restricted partition sum, Paley–Zygmund, then concentration to lift the probability to one half. -> Gaussian gain for sparse overlap measures
- **step-polynomial-coverage** [claim](#stmt-poly-prop-coverage) / [proof](#proof-stmt-poly-lem-coverage-gain-2): Select one violating set, trim the energy tail, apply the gain lemma and the rotation tail. -> polynomial overlap coverage
- **step-coverage-cells** [claim](#stmt-poly-cor-coverage-cells) / [proof](#proof-stmt-poly-cor-coverage-cells): Apply the pair statement to the set of violating targets and derive a contradiction. -> target coverage within subsets
- **step-bank-coverage** [claim](#stmt-fixed-lem-bank-coverage) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fixed-lem-bank-coverage): Union over tuples and indices for bad targets, then independence of the bank for the remaining targets. -> simultaneous bank coverage
- **step-bank-locking** [claim](#stmt-fixed-lem-bank-locking) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fixed-lem-bank-locking): Triples of bank entries and path points have the product Gibbs law; apply absolute locking and a union bound. -> simultaneous bank locking
- **step-nested-crossings** [claim](#stmt-fixed-lem-nested-crossings) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fixed-lem-nested-crossings): Induction over levels using bank coverage and bank locking along the backward search. -> deterministic nesting of crossings
- **step-polynomial-theorem** [claim](#stmt-fixed-thm-polynomial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fixed-lem-nested-crossings-2): Total variation forces a sign passage; nesting reduces it to a shell event, which independent uniform thresholds make unlikely. -> polynomial-time obstruction

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-stretched-exp-obstruction**: At every fixed inverse temperature above one, the realized equilibrium start keeps total-variation distance from equilibrium above one quarter at time exp(n^kappa), with probability tending to one over disorder and the start, for both the continuous-time and the discrete update clocks.
- **inv-superpolynomial-mixing**: For every fixed power of n, the point mixing time from a typical equilibrium start exceeds that power with probability tending to one, in continuous and discrete time.
- **inv-fixed-depth-polynomial**: A second argument with a fixed number of nested crossing levels gives the same polynomial-time obstruction for the distance at time n^a and at floor(n^a) discrete attempts.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-high-temperature**: Citing the obstruction to claim slow mixing of the heat-bath chain at inverse temperature at or below one. -> The proof uses a nontrivial minimizing measure that exists only above one; the statement says nothing about smaller inverse temperatures.
- **anti-worst-case-start**: Reading the result as a bound for an arbitrary or adversarial initial configuration, or as a worst-start mixing time. -> The statement concerns a start drawn from the Gibbs measure and then held fixed; neither mixing time takes a supremum over the initial state.
- **anti-external-field**: Transferring the bound to models with an external field or to other topologies. -> The argument uses the global spin-reversal symmetry of the zero-field dense Gaussian model, which a field would break.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are asymptotic probability statements about random Gibbs configurations: they assert that a probability tends to one as n grows, via concentration, existence of a minimizing measure and union bounds. No finite procedure or explicit construction is given that a program could run and compare to an independent oracle, so Panel 3 does not apply.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main results are probability statements about random disorder and Gibbs mass. They contain no finite procedure that a program could execute and check, so there is no reference implementation to derive. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The rate-one heat-bath clocks describe the dynamics being analysed. The paper proves that such a simulation cannot reach equilibrium quickly from a typical start, but it supplies no simulation scheme or engine design. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The point-wise mixing times could in principle be measured by a diagnostic. The paper only proves asymptotic lower bounds with no explicit constants, so it gives no tool contract that a checker could enforce. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The reference-bank and crossing construction exists only inside the proof, to bound probabilities. The paper never presents it as an algorithm with stated inputs, outputs and cost, so no pseudocode or complexity is claimed. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The banks and retained reference lists are bookkeeping devices for a probabilistic argument. They do not define a data structure with operations or invariants meant for implementation. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no arithmetic kernel, memory layout or parallel scheme. Its statements are asymptotic probability bounds, so nothing maps to an accelerator design. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No primitive, hardness reduction or security game appears in the paper. The result concerns Markov chain mixing in a spin glass, not adversarial security. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The model has independent Gaussian couplings and no data, loss or learning task. Any reading in terms of dense recurrent networks would be an extrapolation the source does not make. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The scalar Parisi functional is minimised only as an analytical tool to obtain pressure comparisons. The paper does not state or analyse an optimisation algorithm or a solver guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The independent Poisson clocks are a modelling device for single-site updates in one chain. There is no network, message passing, consensus or fault model. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural home of this result is the mathematical theory of spin glass dynamics. It is a theory result with no engineering construction, so no practical contract is derived. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-beta-above-one** (hypothesis): The main obstruction is stated only for a fixed inverse temperature above one; constants may depend on the temperature and the argument gives no uniformity as the temperature decreases to one. Blocked use: ['Claiming slow mixing at inverse temperature at or below one', 'Using the bound with a temperature that varies with n']
- **limit-typical-start** (hypothesis): The mixing time is point-wise from a start drawn from the Gibbs measure and then held fixed; no supremum over initial states is taken, so the result is not a worst-case or adversarial-start statement. Blocked use: ['Reading the result as a worst-start mixing time', 'Reordering the conditioning so the kernel is averaged over the start']
- **limit-zero-field** (formal_scope): The model is the zero-field Sherrington-Kirkpatrick model with independent Gaussian couplings; the argument uses invariance of the Gibbs measure under global spin reversal. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to models with an external field', 'Transferring it to sparse or finite-dimensional topologies']
- **limit-exponent-not-optimal** (approximation): The stretched-exponential exponent is not asserted to be optimal, and numerical predictions at other scales are not fixed by this result. Blocked use: ['Treating the exponent as the true relaxation scale', 'Using it as a sharp estimate of the mixing time']
- **limit-single-site-dynamics** (hypothesis): The dynamics are single-site heat-bath updates with rate-one clocks, or uniform-site discrete update attempts; the conclusion is tied to this resampling rule. Blocked use: ['Extending the bound to cluster or non-local update schemes', 'Applying it to different kernels without a new argument']
- **limit-probability-only** (nonconstructive): The conclusions are asymptotic probability statements over disorder and start, using existence of a minimizing measure and union bounds; they give no explicit constants or finite-n guarantee. Blocked use: ['Using the result as a certified finite-size bound', 'Extracting explicit thresholds for a given n']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 227 (`lean/docs/227.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A spectral gap throughout the high-temperature Sherrington–Kirkpatrick phase](../../preprints/A-spectral-gap-throughout-the-high-temperature-Sherrington-Kirkpatrick-phase-September-24-2026/main.pdf)
> - [Cutoff throughout the high-temperature Sherrington–Kirkpatrick phase](../../preprints/Cutoff-throughout-the-high-temperature-Sherrington-Kirkpatrick-phase-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Critical slowing down in the Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model](../../preprints/Critical-slowing-down-in-the-Sherrington-Kirkpatrick-model-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Stretched-exponential barriers for typical SK initial states](../../preprints/Stretched-exponential-barriers-for-typical-SK-initial-states-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> For every fixed inverse temperature $0<\beta<1$, the formalization proves a dimension-independent Poincaré inequality for the zero-field Gaussian Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model with probability tending to one over the disorder. Equivalently, the unscaled single-site heat-bath spectral gap stays bounded away from zero. For dynamics that choose one site uniformly at each discrete step, the spectral gap is at least $1/(Cn)$ with probability tending to one, for a constant $C$ depending on $\beta$.
>
> The formalized supporting result proves ratio cutoff for discrete single-site heat-bath dynamics in the zero-field Gaussian Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model for $0\le\beta<1/2$. For every $0<\varepsilon<1/2$ and $\eta>0$, the probability over the disorder that $t_{\mathrm{mix}}(\varepsilon)/t_{\mathrm{mix}}(1-\varepsilon)>1+\eta$ tends to zero as $n\to\infty$. The denominator is positive for all sufficiently large sizes.
>
> The paper's full range $\beta<1$, explicit cutoff location, and continuous-time conclusion are outside this selected statement.
>
> At criticality in the zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model, the formalization proves that for every fixed $\varepsilon>0$, the continuous-time mixing time lies between $n^{2/3-\varepsilon}$ and $e^{\varepsilon n}$, and the discrete-time mixing count lies between $n^{5/3-\varepsilon}$ and $e^{\varepsilon n}$, with probability tending to one over the Gaussian disorder. The disorder has independent off-diagonal entries of variance $1/n$. Continuous updates have rate one per site; discrete time counts uniformly chosen single-site update attempts. Mixing is worst-start total variation at threshold $1/4$, with holding allowed.
>
> For deterministic times $t_n=o(n^{2/3})$ or update counts $k_n=o(n^{5/3})$, the Gibbs mass of realized starting states still farther than $1/4$ from equilibrium tends to one in probability. For every deterministic positive sequence $M_n\to\infty$, the covariance operator norm and the linear Rayleigh supremum, using the unscaled heat-bath Dirichlet form, both lie between $n^{2/3}/M_n$ and $M_n n^{2/3}$ with probability tending to one. The covariance result does not give an upper bound for the full inverse spectral gap.
>
> The formalization proves a stretched-exponential mixing obstruction for the zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model at every fixed inverse temperature $\beta>1$. At time $\exp(n^{1/10000})$, the expected Gibbs mass of initial configurations whose total-variation distance from equilibrium exceeds $1/4$ tends to one. Since this mass lies in $[0,1]$, it also tends to one in probability over the disorder.
>
> The result covers both rate-one-per-site continuous-time heat-bath dynamics and the same stated number of discrete update attempts.

Comparator source `SKBarriers.lean` (2976 bytes, sha256 `70b833ec6c74952f52c215767593347afd89bfa8d668b0b57792f19c19cc515a`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

section

section

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators Topology
open MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory Filter

namespace SK
abbrev Config (n : ℕ) := Fin n → Bool
abbrev Edge (n : ℕ) := {p : Fin n × Fin n // p.1 < p.2}
abbrev Disorder (n : ℕ) := Edge n → ℝ

def spin (b : Bool) : ℝ := if b then 1 else -1

def hamiltonian {n : ℕ} (J : Disorder n) (x : Config n) : ℝ :=
  (∑ e : Edge n, J e * spin (x e.val.1) * spin (x e.val.2)) / Real.sqrt n

def partition {n : ℕ} (β : ℝ) (J : Disorder n) : ℝ :=
  ∑ x : Config n, Real.exp (β * hamiltonian J x)

def gibbs {n : ℕ} (β : ℝ) (J : Disorder n) (x : Config n) : ℝ :=
  Real.exp (β * hamiltonian J x) / partition β J

def coupling {n : ℕ} (J : Disorder n) (i j : Fin n) : ℝ :=
  if h : i < j then J ⟨(i, j), h⟩ else
    if h : j < i then J ⟨(j, i), h⟩ else 0

def localField {n : ℕ} (J : Disorder n) (x : Config n) (i : Fin n) : ℝ :=
  (∑ j : Fin n, coupling J i j * spin (x j)) / Real.sqrt n

def heatBath {n : ℕ} (β : ℝ) (J : Disorder n) (x y : Config n) : ℝ :=
  (∑ i : Fin n, if Function.update x i (y i) = y then
    Real.exp (β * spin (y i) * localField J x i) /
      (2 * Real.cosh (β * localField J x i)) else 0) / n

def discreteKernel {n : ℕ} (β : ℝ) (J : Disorder n) : ℕ → Config n → Config n → ℝ
  | 0, x, y => if x = y then 1 else 0
  | k + 1, x, y => ∑ z : Config n, heatBath β J x z * discreteKernel β J k z y

def continuousKernel {n : ℕ} (β : ℝ) (J : Disorder n) (t : ℝ)
    (x y : Config n) : ℝ :=
  ∑' k : ℕ, (Real.exp (-(n : ℝ) * t) * ((n : ℝ) * t) ^ k /
    (Nat.factorial k : ℝ)) * discreteKernel β J k x y

def discreteDistance {n : ℕ} (β : ℝ) (J : Disorder n)
    (x : Config n) (k : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  (1 / 2 : ℝ) * ∑ y : Config n, |discreteKernel β J k x y - gibbs β J y|

def continuousDistance {n : ℕ} (β : ℝ) (J : Disorder n)
    (x : Config n) (t : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (1 / 2 : ℝ) * ∑ y : Config n, |continuousKernel β J t x y - gibbs β J y|

def disorderLaw (n : ℕ) : Measure (Disorder n) :=
  Measure.pi (fun _ : Edge n => gaussianReal 0 1)

def kappa : ℝ := 1 / 10000

def timeScale (n : ℕ) : ℝ := Real.exp ((n : ℝ) ^ kappa)

def continuousBadMass (β : ℝ) (n : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  ∫ J : Disorder n, (∑ x : Config n, if 1 / 4 < continuousDistance β J x (timeScale n)
    then gibbs β J x else 0) ∂disorderLaw n

def discreteBadMass (β : ℝ) (n : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  ∫ J : Disorder n, (∑ x : Config n, if 1 / 4 < discreteDistance β J x ⌊timeScale n⌋₊
    then gibbs β J x else 0) ∂disorderLaw n

end SK
end
end
end

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators Topology
open MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory Filter

namespace SK

theorem main (β : ℝ) (hβ : 1 < β) :
    Tendsto (continuousBadMass β) atTop (𝓝 1) ∧
    Tendsto (discreteBadMass β) atTop (𝓝 1) := by
  sorry

end SK
end

end OAI
```

For every deterministic positive sequence $M_n\\to\\infty$, the covariance operator norm and the linear Rayleigh supremum, using the unscaled heat-bath Dirichlet form, both lie between $n^{2/3}/M_n$ and $M_n n^{2/3}$ with probability tending to one. The covariance result does not give an upper bound for the full inverse spectral gap.\n\nThe formalization proves a stretched-exponential mixing obstruction for the zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model at every fixed inverse temperature $\\beta>1$. At time $\\exp(n^{1/10000})$, the expected Gibbs mass of initial configurations whose total-variation distance from equilibrium exceeds $1/4$ tends to one. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


At every fixed inverse temperature <i>β</i> &gt; 1, we prove a stretched-exponential obstruction to mixing for the zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model from typical equilibrium configurations held fixed as initial states. The Gibbs mass of states whose total-variation distance from equilibrium at time $`\exp(n^{1/10000})`$ exceeds 1/4 tends to one in probability over the disorder. This holds for rate-one-per-site heat-bath dynamics and after the same stated number of discrete update attempts.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Local spin updates can preserve information about a realized equilibrium configuration for a long time, even though the unconditioned chain is stationary. We quantify this persistence throughout the low-temperature phase of the zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model. The central issue is the mass of slowly mixing initial states: a worst-state lower bound alone gives no information about whether a Gibbs sample encounters the obstruction.

<a id="eq:model"></a>
For an integer $n\ge2$, let $\Sigma_n=\{-1,1\}^n$, and let $(J_{ij})_{1\le i<j\le n}$ be independent standard Gaussian variables. At inverse temperature $\beta>0$, the zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model has Hamiltonian and Gibbs measure $$\label{eq:model}
 H^J(x)=H_n^J(x)=\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{i<j}J_{ij}x_ix_j,
 \qquad
 \pi^J(x)=\pi_{n,\beta}^J(x)
       =\frac{e^{\beta H^J(x)}}{Z_{n,\beta}^J},
 \qquad x\in\Sigma_n,$$ where $Z^J=Z_{n,\beta}^J=\sum_{x\in\Sigma_n}e^{\beta H^J(x)}$. For $x,y\in\Sigma_n$, their overlap is $$R(x,y)=\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n x_i y_i.$$ The measure $\pi^J$ is invariant under the global spin reversal $x\mapsto-x$. We fix $\beta>1$ before sending $n$ to infinity.

In continuous-time heat-bath dynamics, every site has an independent rate-one clock. At a ring of site $i$, its spin is resampled according to $$\mathbb P(x_i\gets s\mid x_{[n]\setminus\{i\}})
  =\frac{\exp\{\beta s h_i^J(x)\}}
         {2\cosh(\beta h_i^J(x))},
 \qquad
 h_i^J(x)=\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{j\ne i}J_{ij}x_j,
 \quad s\in\{-1,1\},$$ with $J_{ji}=J_{ij}$ and $J_{ii}=0$. Write $P_t^J$ for its transition kernel. The total update-attempt rate is $n$, and an attempt may leave the configuration unchanged. We also consider the discrete kernel $K^J$ that chooses one site uniformly and performs one such conditional resampling. Both dynamics have stationary measure $\pi^J$.

For probability measures $\nu,\eta$ on $\Sigma_n$, set $\lVert \nu-\eta\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}=\frac12\sum_x|\nu(x)-\eta(x)|$. For a fixed realized state $x$, define <a id="eq:point-mixing-time"></a><a id="eq:point-discrete-mixing-time"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 d^J(x,t)&=\lVert P_t^J(x,\cdot)-\pi^J\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}},
 &T^J(x)&=\inf\{t\ge0:d^J(x,t)\le1/4\},
 \label{eq:point-mixing-time}\\
 d_{\mathrm{disc}}^J(x,k)&=
      \lVert (K^J)^k(x,\cdot)-\pi^J\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}},
 &T_{\mathrm{disc}}^J(x)&=
      \min\{k\ge0:d_{\mathrm{disc}}^J(x,k)\le1/4\}.
 \label{eq:point-discrete-mixing-time}
\end{aligned}$$ Here $k$ is an integer, and neither mixing time takes a supremum over the initial state.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Set $\kappa=1/10000$. For every fixed $\beta>1$, $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathbb P_{J,\,x_0\sim\pi^J}
  \left\{d^J\bigl(x_0,\exp(n^\kappa)\bigr)>\frac14\right\}
  &\longrightarrow1,\\
 \mathbb P_{J,\,x_0\sim\pi^J}
  \left\{d_{\mathrm{disc}}^J
       \bigl(x_0,\lfloor\exp(n^\kappa)\rfloor\bigr)>\frac14\right\}
  &\longrightarrow1.
\end{aligned}$$*

</div>

The order of conditioning in this statement is essential. We first draw $x_0$ from $\pi^J$, then regard it as a fixed argument of the transition kernel. Integrating that kernel over $x_0$ would instead recover $\pi^J$ at every time. The joint formulation above is equivalent to convergence of the corresponding Gibbs mass to one in disorder probability. Indeed, if $F_n(J)\in[0,1]$ is the mass of the complementary set, the joint assertion says $\mathbb EF_n\to0$. Markov’s inequality gives convergence in probability, and boundedness proves the converse.

Total variation contracts under a Markov kernel. In particular, for $s,t\ge0$, $$d^J(x,t+s)
  =\lVert \bigl(P_t^J(x,\cdot)-\pi^J\bigr)P_s^J\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}
  \le d^J(x,t),$$ and the discrete distances are nonincreasing in $k$ as well. Since the state space is finite, $t\mapsto d^J(x,t)$ is continuous. Consequently $d^J(x,t)>1/4$ implies $T^J(x)>t$; in discrete time, $d_{\mathrm{disc}}^J(x,k)>1/4$ implies $T_{\mathrm{disc}}^J(x)>k$. It follows that the theorem implies a lower bound at every fixed polynomial time scale. Appendix <a href="#fixed-sec:crossings" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fixed-sec:crossings">10</a> will also give an independent proof using a fixed number of crossing levels.

<a id="stmt-cor-superpolynomial"></a>
<div id="cor:superpolynomial" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *For every fixed $\beta>1$ and every fixed $a>0$, $$\mathbb P_{J,\,x_0\sim\pi^J}\{T^J(x_0)>n^a\}\longrightarrow1,
 \qquad
 \mathbb P_{J,\,x_0\sim\pi^J}
       \{T_{\mathrm{disc}}^J(x_0)>n^a\}\longrightarrow1.$$ Equivalently, each corresponding Gibbs mass tends to one in probability over the disorder.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-superpolynomial"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-superpolynomial</summary>

*Proof.* Fix $a>0$. Eventually $n^a$ is smaller than both $\exp(n^\kappa)$ and $\lfloor\exp(n^\kappa)\rfloor$. Apply Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> at those larger times and use contraction of total variation to pass to $n^a$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Background and relation to earlier work

Sherrington and Kirkpatrick introduced the model as a mean-field description of a spin glass <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[SK75](#ref-SK75)]. Parisi’s variational description of its equilibrium free energy <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Parisi80](#ref-Parisi80)] was established rigorously through Guerra’s interpolation bound and Talagrand’s matching lower bound for the limiting pressure <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Guerra03](#ref-Guerra03), [Talagrand06](#ref-Talagrand06)]. These equilibrium results provide the scalar variational structure behind our argument. Passing from free energy to relaxation requires additional information about which overlap configurations a local trajectory can visit.

Ben Arous and Jagannath developed criteria that turn overlap free-energy barriers into exponentially small spectral gaps for mean-field spin glasses <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[BAJ18](#ref-BAJ18)]. Their use of constrained replica pressures is an important antecedent of the overlap comparisons used here. Their barrier criteria require additional landscape hypotheses; the explicit Ising sufficient conditions in that work exclude the pure quadratic covariance of SK. They therefore do not directly give the present result throughout $\beta>1$. For SK itself, Sellke proves exponentially slow worst-case Glauber mixing at sufficiently large inverse temperature <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Sellke25](#ref-Sellke25), Theorem 1.1]. That conclusion concerns the slowest initial states. It does not determine the Gibbs mass of the states that remain far from equilibrium after their initial configuration has been fixed.

The role of the external field is also substantial. Bandeira, El Alaoui, and Rödder prove polynomial worst-case mixing for lazy discrete heat-bath dynamics at every fixed inverse temperature when a sufficiently large constant uniform field is present, with high probability over the disorder <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BER26](#ref-BER26), Corollary 1.2]. Our result concerns zero field. Its distinctive combination is the entire range of fixed $\beta>1$, Gibbs mass tending to one for realized initial states, and a stretched-exponential time scale. The exponent $1/10000$ is not asserted to be optimal, and the constants in the proof may depend on $\beta$; the argument does not give uniformity as $\beta$ decreases to one.

El Alaoui, Montanari and Sellke prove a complementary obstruction to sampling at $\beta>1$ for algorithms stable under disorder perturbations, in normalized $2$-Wasserstein distance <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[AMS22](#ref-AMS22), Theorem 2.6]. Their stability hypothesis and approximation metric differ from the conditional Glauber transition law considered here.

Numerical studies have examined much sharper predictions for low-temperature relaxation. Billoire studies equilibrium autocorrelation times for binary couplings and sequential Metropolis updates <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Billoire10](#ref-Billoire10)]; Monthus and Garel compute inverse spectral gaps for small SK systems with Metropolis dynamics <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[MonthusGarel09](#ref-MonthusGarel09)]. Both report evidence associated with an $n^{1/3}$ scale for logarithmic relaxation times. Their observables and dynamics differ from the state-conditioned total variation distance studied here, and these numerical predictions do not fix the exponent in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

The static comparisons build on Guerra’s Gaussian interpolation framework <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Guerra03](#ref-Guerra03)] and Talagrand’s extensions to systems with prescribed replica overlaps <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Talagrand07](#ref-Talagrand07), Propositions 3.4 and 4.4]. Panchenko developed a general vector-spin formulation and proved the asymptotic sharpness of these constrained-overlap bounds <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Panchenko18](#ref-Panchenko18)]. Talagrand’s two- and three-copy bounds already retain an explicit cost when the prescribed overlaps differ from the covariance endpoint. In Section <a href="#static-sec:gaussian" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="static-sec:gaussian">2</a>, we derive the finite-hierarchy matrix form needed here for the quadratic SK covariance, allowing arbitrary finite replica dimension and nonunit endpoint diagonals. The same section obtains a quantitative scalar free-energy comparison by choosing an interpolation path through a differential equation driven by overlap expectations. This choice has a methodological parallel in the adaptive interpolation method of Barbier and Macris <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BarbierMacris19](#ref-BarbierMacris19)]; their theorems are not inputs to our finite-size SK estimate. Gaussian concentration and the Brascamp–Lieb variance inequality <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[BL76](#ref-BL76)] supply the probabilistic estimates used in these comparisons.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof strategy

Set $L=n^\kappa$ and $\rho=L^{-1}$. Our task is to exclude an overlap sign passage during the first $e^L$ units of time. To connect that task to mixing, choose a reference $v_1$ with $R(v_1,x_0)\ge q_*$ for a fixed $q_*>0$. The half-space $\{z:R(v_1,z)\le0\}$ has Gibbs mass at least $1/2$ by spin reversal. A transition law within $1/4$ of equilibrium must therefore enter it with probability at least $1/4$. The reference will be chosen from independent equilibrium samples, using the initial state but no future updates.

The first part of the paper establishes the static estimates that make this path argument possible. Section <a href="#static-sec:gaussian" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="static-sec:gaussian">2</a> compares the expected normalized log partition function $n^{-1}\mathbb E\log Z^J$ with the minimum of the scalar Parisi functional, with error $O_\beta(n^{-1/24})$, and proves the matrix comparison for constrained replica overlaps.
